# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua works on LSATU teaching, student records, curriculum quality, releases, and a Windows/AI-tool environment. He uses Codex for evidence-based implementation, independent review, connected-app drafting, recovery, and live-system checks. He values concept-first teaching, plain language for nontechnical workflows, and a hard distinction between source/static evidence and deployment or signed-in acceptance.

## User preferences

- Preserve strict scope: honor "Do not commit, push or publish," keep edits in the named path, and report deviations or blocked checks explicitly.
- Validate behavior, not just prose: make predictions, run broken and fixed cases, retain failure evidence/receipts, and distinguish direct proof from inference.
- Do not fabricate browser, device, deployment, persistence, permission, or acceptance evidence; keep tickets open until the requested paths are actually evidenced.
- For student lessons, teach the exact recorded sticking point first; use visual ordinary-language explanation before notation and preserve modality, complete conditions, and fact-versus-rule distinctions.
- When reusing student work, locate and assign the exact existing artifact rather than recreating or paraphrasing it; use a dry run and read-after-write verification for live assignments.
- Use the Codex in-app browser for LSAT Lab checks. Respect explicit approval gates; pilot approval does not authorize a later load.
- For shared Windows tools, diagnose before reinstalling and document availability for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity. Use separate PowerShell commands when requested: "ONE COMMAND PER CALL."
- For connected-app drafting, "Show the draft here; do not send, post, or modify anything." Match real demonstrated style.
- For research synthesis, retain only claims corroborated across two independent sources, each with an exact quote and source.

## General Tips

- Separate focused test/typecheck/build, deployment, and fresh signed-in acceptance; static approval is not visual, keyboard, interaction, or publication proof.
- Resume work by reconciling handoffs with current diffs, live configuration/database pulls, and `wiki/hot.md`; stale checkouts and status claims are recurring hazards.
- Database content writes: snapshot first, reconcile overlapping proposals, guard exact old text/MD5, use approved direct transport, then server-side audit. Do not hand-type huge SQL payloads.
- Windows `command not found` can be stale inherited PATH: check executable/WinGet/saved PATH, refresh or restart the app before reinstalling.
- Preserve unrelated edits, local conflicts, recovery data, secrets, and active browser/session state. GitHub parity is not laptop parity.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"`.</multi_agent_role>

## developer

<multi_agent_mode>Any earlier instruction enabling proactive multi-agent delegation no longer applies. Do not spawn sub-agents unless the user or applicable AGENTS.md/skill instructions explicitly ask for sub-agents, delegation, or parallel agent work.</multi_agent_mode>

A blanket instruction to take the immediately following words is too broad. |\\r\\n| Historical intro and its review | `research/conditional-homework-2026-10-02/1991094de0c213db_Conditional intro.pdf.txt`; `research/2026-10-02-conditional-homework-source-review.md:52–63` | The four-case chart conflicts with accompanying prose. Several captures are truncated. Outdated rule-disappears prose and conflicting lists do not override the selected standard. Quantity, Groups 3/4 and necessary-assumption material are outside this page. |\\r\\n\\r\\nNo source named Savim surfaced in the routed inventory. The 7Sage source was located and reviewed; those names are not silently treated as equivalent. Demon clips have incomplete speaker/publication provenance. This review covers the relevant sources found through the corpus and source routes, not every unindexed file on the machine.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Each part has a teaching job\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s wording follow-up on 2026-10-10 makes the opening link explicit: whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too. The example now includes then, identifies being a student as its required part and uses you are rather than you\'re. The limit on what a requirement establishes follows the definition and worked example. The Alan Scott mention was a dictation error; Joshua clarified that he meant LSAT sources. The copy follow-up passed 15 relevant tests and was verified in Codex Browser; the full build receipt below covers the preceding implementation.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Define required and distinguish that label from enough.\\r\\n2. Revisit enrollment/studenthood, keeping the required relationship explicit.\\r\\n3. Use a possible history student to show why studenthood alone does not establish enrollment.\\r\\n4. Compare studenthood being present or absent. The visual leaves enrollment possible when present and rules it out when absent. It does not announce a real person\'s enrollment or teach the named contrapositive procedure.\\r\\n5. Show the reviewed seven Group 2 forms: only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must. The original HTML\'s longer inventory remains separate in the existing supplementary family.\\r\\n6. Use a fresh current-reference-letter rule. One question preserves the qualifier; the other distinguishes a requirement from a guarantee. Both have reasoned wrong-answer feedback and retry recovery.\\r\\n7. Bridge to the existing must-arrow lesson. Formal contraposition, extended indicator lessons and foundation drills retain their destinations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification\\r\\n\\r\\n- 21 focused tests pass, including requirement present/absent, persistent live feedback, correct/wrong answers and retry focus.\\r\\n- Full application typecheck passes.\\r\\n- Codex Browser verifies keyboard activation, both visual states, both answer paths, retries and next/previous navigation.\\r\\n- Actual CSS widths 320, 400, 960, 1200 and 1600: no document overflow or lesson buttons below 44px. Viewport override reset. Palette applies to visible definition pages and does not leak into the must-arrow page.\\r\\n- Full `npm run build` and postbuild exclusion check pass. The final bundle contains the corrected required-versus-enough definition. Receipt: `necessary-lesson-build.log`.\\r\\n- No physical-device, screen-reader speech or production acceptance claim.\\r\\nimport { useId, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { sufficientConditionFilms } from \'../content/sufficientConditionFilms\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type SufficientConditionFilmAsset = { id: string; title: string; src: string; poster?: string; captions: string; caption: string; transcript: string };\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Render only an actual generated asset; the written lesson remains available without video. */\\r\\nexport function SufficientConditionFilm({ assets = sufficientConditionFilms }: { assets?: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[] }) {\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const [failedId, setFailedId] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const videoId = useId();\\r\\n  const asset = assets[selected];\\r\\n  if (!asset) return null;\\r\\n  const failed = failedId === asset.id;\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"sc-film\\" aria-label=\\"Short explanations of sufficiency\\">\\r\\n    <header><p className=\\"sc-eyebrow\\">Watch the explanation</p><h3>What the rule gives us</h3></header>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"sc-film-selector\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Choose an explanation\\">\\r\\n      {assets.map((clip, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={clip.id}\\r\\n        aria-pressed={index === selected} aria-controls={videoId}\\r\\n        onClick={() => { setSelected(index); setFailedId(null); }}>{clip.title}</button>)}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <figure>\\r\\n      <video id={videoId} key={`${asset.id}-${attempt}`} controls preload=\\"metadata\\" playsInline\\r\\n        poster={asset.poster} aria-label={asset.caption} onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}>\\r\\n        <source src={asset.src} type=\\"video/mp4\\" onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}/>\\r\\n        <track kind=\\"captions\\" src={asset.captions} srcLang=\\"en\\" label=\\"English\\" default/>\\r\\n        Your browser cannot play this clip. The explanation is available below.\\r\\n      </video>\\r\\n      <figcaption>{asset.caption}</figcaption>\\r\\n    </figure>\\r\\n    {failed && <div className=\\"sc-film-error\\">\\r\\n      <p role=\\"alert\\">The video couldn\'t load. You can read the explanation below or try again.</p>\\r\\n      <button type=\\"button\\" onClick={() => { setFailedId(null); setAttempt(value => value + 1); }}>Try loading the video again</button>\\r\\n    </div>}\\r\\n    <details key={`transcript-${asset.id}-${failed}`} open={failed || undefined}>\\r\\n      <summary>Read the video explanation</summary><p>{asset.transcript}</p>\\r\\n    </details>\\r\\n  </section>;\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport type { SufficientConditionFilmAsset } from \'../components/SufficientConditionFilm\';\\r\\n\\r\\n// Add only generated clips whose picture, narration and captions have been reviewed.\\r\\nexport const sufficientConditionFilms: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[] = [\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'enough-to-know\',\\r\\n    title: \'Enough to know\',\\r\\n    src: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/enough-to-know.mp4\',\\r\\n    poster: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/enough-to-know.jpg\',\\r\\n    captions: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/enough-to-know.vtt\',\\r\\n    caption: \\"Knowing Maya is enrolled in law school is enough to know she\'s a student.\\",\\r\\n    transcript: \\"A sufficient condition is enough to guarantee something else. If Maya is enrolled in law school, she\'s a student. Knowing she\'s enrolled is enough. We don\'t need another fact.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    id: \'what-stays-open\',\\r\\n    title: \'What stays open\',\\r\\n    src: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/what-stays-open.mp4\',\\r\\n    poster: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/what-stays-open.jpg\',\\r\\n    captions: \'/media/conditionals/sufficiency/what-stays-open.vtt\',\\r\\n    caption: \\"Knowing Maya is a student doesn\'t tell us whether she\'s enrolled in law school.\\",\\r\\n    transcript: \\"Now, suppose all we know is that Maya is a student. Is she enrolled in law school? We don\'t know. She could be studying history. Being a student doesn\'t tell us which subject she studies.\\",\\r\\n  },\\r\\n];\\r\\n"}'}]

That is the combination this guarantee rules out.\\",\\r\\n    caption: \'An enrolled person outside Students would break the rule.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  {\\r\\n    key: \'other\', label: \'A student outside law school\', fits: true,\\r\\n    result: \'Without law-school enrollment, you might be a student or you might not.\',\\r\\n    explanation: \\"Someone not enrolled in law school could be a student or not a student. The highlight shows one allowed possibility.\\",\\r\\n    caption: \'Highlighted: one possible case, a student outside law school.\',\\r\\n  },\\r\\n] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction StudentSymbol({ x, y }: { x: number; y: number }) {\\r\\n  return <g transform={`translate(${x} ${y})`}>\\r\\n    <circle cy=\\"-10\\" r=\\"7\\"/>\\r\\n    <path d=\\"M-12 15v-5a12 12 0 0 1 24 0v5Z\\"/>\\r\\n  </g>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function SufficientConditionExperience() {\\r\\n  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);\\r\\n  const titleId = useId();\\r\\n  const diagramId = useId();\\r\\n  const readingId = useId();\\r\\n  const current = relationships[selected];\\r\\n  return <section className=\\"sc-experience\\" aria-labelledby={titleId}>\\r\\n    <header>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"sc-eyebrow\\">Picture the relationship</p>\\r\\n      <h3 id={titleId}>Law-school students belong inside the student group.</h3>\\r\\n      <p>Choose a combination and compare it with the groups. Does it fit the guarantee?</p>\\r\\n    </header>\\r\\n    <figure className=\\"sc-membership\\" data-case={current.key}>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"sc-group-visual\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"sc-group-label\\"><span>Students</span><span className=\\"sc-group-note\\">The larger group</span></div>\\r\\n        <svg className=\\"sc-group-drawing\\" viewBox=\\"0 0 600 310\\" role=\\"img\\" aria-labelledby={diagramId}>\\r\\n          <title id={diagramId}>{current.key === \'contradiction\' ? \'An enrolled person outside the student group is crossed out: this combination breaks the guarantee.\' : current.key === \'other\' ? \'Students outside law school are highlighted inside the student group: one allowed possibility. Someone outside law school could also be outside both groups.\' : \'The entire law-school group is highlighted inside the student group.\'}</title>\\r\\n          <rect className=\\"sc-student-boundary\\" x=\\"8\\" y=\\"8\\" width=\\"584\\" height=\\"234\\" rx=\\"30\\"/>\\r\\n          <rect className=\\"sc-law-boundary\\" x=\\"30\\" y=\\"30\\" width=\\"270\\" height=\\"190\\" rx=\\"24\\"/>\\r\\n          <g className=\\"sc-law-symbols\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">\\r\\n            <StudentSymbol x={86} y={88}/><StudentSymbol x={165} y={88}/><StudentSymbol x={244} y={88}/>\\r\\n            <StudentSymbol x={86} y={162}/><StudentSymbol x={165} y={162}/><StudentSymbol x={244} y={162}/>\\r\\n          </g>\\r\\n          <g className=\\"sc-other-symbols\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">\\r\\n            <StudentSymbol x={365} y={88}/><StudentSymbol x={445} y={88}/><StudentSymbol x={525} y={88}/>\\r\\n            <StudentSymbol x={365} y={162}/><StudentSymbol x={445} y={162}/><StudentSymbol x={525} y={162}/>\\r\\n          </g>\\r\\n          {current.key === \'contradiction\' && <g className=\\"sc-excluded-person\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">\\r\\n            <path d=\\"M165 220v42h165\\" className=\\"sc-exclusion-guide\\"/>\\r\\n            <StudentSymbol x={370} y={275}/>\\r\\n            <circle cx=\\"370\\" cy=\\"275\\" r=\\"29\\"/>\\r\\n            <path d=\\"m350 255 40 40\\"/>\\r\\n            <text x=\\"415\\" y=\\"279\\">Excluded</text>\\r\\n          </g>}\\r\\n          {current.key !== \'contradiction\' && <g className=\\"sc-diagram-annotation\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">\\r\\n            <path d={current.key === \'other\' ? \'M445 190v63\' : \'M165 190v63\'}/>\\r\\n            <text x={current.key === \'other\' ? 445 : 165} y=\\"279\\" textAnchor=\\"middle\\">{current.key === \'other\' ? \'Allowed too\' : \'Always inside\'}</text>\\r\\n          </g>}\\r\\n        </svg>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"sc-diagram-key\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\"><span><i/>Enrolled in law school</span><span><i/>Other students</span></div>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n      <figcaption>{current.caption} Figures show the groups.</figcaption>\\r\\n    </figure>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"sc-case-controls\\" role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Compare combinations with this conditional\\">\\r\\n      {relationships.map((relationship, index) => <button type=\\"button\\" key={relationship.key} aria-pressed={selected === index} aria-controls={readingId} onClick={() => setSelected(index)}>{relationship.label}</button>)}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"sc-case-reading\\" id={readingId} aria-live=\\"polite\\" aria-atomic=\\"true\\" data-fits={current.fits}>\\r\\n      <p className=\\"sc-case-verdict\\"><span aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{current.fits ? \'✓\' : \'×\'}</span>{current.fits ? \'Allowed by this conditional\' : \'Breaks the guarantee\'}</p>\\r\\n      <strong>{current.result}</strong>\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ccba2f","wall_time_seconds":0.3906942,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3769,"output":"        <p className=\\"cr-sufficiency-lead\\"><strong>Sufficient means enough.</strong> When one condition guarantees another, knowing the first is true tells you the second has to be true too. The first is the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>: it\'s enough to establish the second.</p>\\r\\n        <p>You don\'t need to meet an additional condition to get that guarantee. And we\'re talking about certainty, not likelihood: the first condition can\'t be true while the condition it guarantees is false.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Let\'s look at what that means:</p>\\r\\n        <blockquote><strong>If</strong> you\'re enrolled in law school, you\'re a student.</blockquote>\\r\\n        <p>Being enrolled in law school is enough to know you\'re <strong>a student</strong>. You don\'t need to know which classes you\'re taking. Enrollment already guarantees it. That\'s why being enrolled is the sufficient condition in this relationship.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Here, <strong>if</strong> introduces <strong>being enrolled in law school</strong>. That whole idea is the sufficient condition. If is the word that helps you find it. We haven\'t said you\'re actually enrolled; we\'re explaining what follows if you are.</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation\\" aria-label=\\"What makes a condition sufficient\\">\\r\\n          <SufficientReasoningWalkthrough/>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation\\" aria-label=\\"When the sufficient condition is not met\\">\\r\\n          <h3>What if you aren\'t enrolled in law school?</h3>\\r\\n          <p>You could be studying something else. Or you might not be a student at all. Both are possible. Without law-school enrollment, this conditional doesn\'t tell us whether you\'re a student.</p>\\r\\n          <p>That\'s the difference: <strong>being enrolled guarantees being a student; not being enrolled leaves the question open.</strong></p>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <SufficientConditionExperience/>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation sc-indicator-list\\" aria-label=\\"Group 1 indicator words\\">\\r\\n          <h3>Group 1: Sufficient-condition indicators</h3>\\r\\n          <p>You\'ve seen how <strong>if</strong> points to the sufficient condition. These indicator words and phrases can help you recognize it too:</p>\\r\\n          <ul>{sufficientFamily.map(entry => <li key={entry.word}>{entry.word}</li>)}</ul>\\r\\n          <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is a word or phrase that helps you recognize the relationship. In our example, <strong>if</strong> is the indicator; <strong>being enrolled in law school</strong> is the condition that guarantees being a student. Look for the words, then ask what they tell you about the two conditions.</p>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation cr-sufficient-checks\\" aria-label=\\"Check your understanding of sufficiency\\">\\r\\n          <h3>Use an indicator to find the guarantee</h3>\\r\\n          <p>Let\'s use <strong>every</strong> in a new example:</p>\\r\\n          <blockquote><strong>Every</strong> applicant invited to an interview receives an email.</blockquote>\\r\\n          <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Which condition guarantees receiving an email?\\" options={[\'Being an applicant invited to an interview.\', \'Being an applicant.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Every covers the applicants invited to an interview. For anyone in that group, receiving an email is guaranteed.\', \'The rule covers applicants invited to an interview, not all applicants. Keep invited to an interview with applicant; that is what guarantees the email.\']}/>\\r\\n          <h3>What does the rule leave open?</h3>\\r\\n          <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Does this rule tell us whether applicants who aren\'t invited receive an email?\\" options={[\'Yes. They cannot receive an email.\', \'No. They could receive an email, or they might not.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'The rule guarantees an email for invited applicants. It does not say that only invited applicants receive emails. Others could receive a different email, or no email at all.\', \'The rule tells us what invited applicants receive. It says nothing about emails to other applicants, so either possibility fits.\']}/>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n        <aside className=\\"cr-sufficient-bridge\\"><h3>Next: The Necessary Condition</h3><p>We\'ve focused on what guarantees being a student: enrollment in law school. Now look at the relationship from the other side. You can\'t be enrolled in law school without being a student. Being a student is the <strong>necessary condition</strong>, the part that must be true. That\'s what we\'ll examine next.</p></aside>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n      <p>Next, we\'ll examine what the sufficient condition requires.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={3} label=\\"The sufficient family\\" title=\\"Different words can preserve the same rule\\">\\r\\n      <p>An <strong>indicator</strong> is an expression that helps you recognize a conditional relationship. These expressions can introduce an idea that is enough to establish another.</p>\\r\\n      <p>For the next examples, use a different stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. We will express that same connection in several ways. We haven\'t said everyone with a key borrows a locker.</p>\\r\\n      <WordFamily entries={sufficientFamily} role=\\"sufficient\\"/>\\r\\n      <p>When/whenever and any/anytime group related forms together. An ending changes the kind of case being discussed, but these examples keep the same connection. For example, every guest wearing a blue badge covers that whole group. Guest alone would include people without badges. Use the expression to find the group, then keep the words that limit it.</p>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Every guest wearing a blue badge receives a lunch voucher. Which complete idea is enough?\\" options={[\'Guest\', \'Guest wearing a blue badge\', \'Receives a lunch voucher\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'Guest alone drops the badge restriction and would make a claim about unbadged guests.\', \'The rule gives a voucher to every guest wearing a blue badge. Keep that qualification with the person.\', \'The voucher is what the rule establishes. It has not said every voucher recipient wears a blue badge.\']}/>\\r\\n      <details className=\\"cr-extension\\"><summary>Two more Group 1 expressions</summary><p><strong>Where</strong> can state a relationship across locations: “Where a collection point operates, parcels can be returned.” An operating collection point establishes the availability of returns there. Availability is not proof that someone made a return.</p><p><strong>The only</strong> needs its complete phrase: “The only notebooks on this shelf are hardback.” A notebook on the shelf is hardback. The sentence does not put every hardback notebook there.</p><p>These are part of your Group 1 work. Understand what the full expression covers before using its group as a placement shortcut.</p></details>\\r\\n      <p>We have seen what can be enough. Now let\'s name what that condition requires.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={4} label=\\"The necessary condition\\" title=\\"Necessary means required\\">\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-sufficiency nc-lesson\\">\\r\\n        <p className=\\"cr-sufficiency-lead\\"><strong>Necessary means required.</strong> In a conditional relationship, the <strong>necessary condition</strong> is what the sufficient condition requires. Whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too. It cannot be missing.</p>\\r\\n        <p>Let\'s return to the relationship from the last lesson:</p>\\r\\n        <blockquote>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are <strong>a student</strong>.</blockquote>\\r\\n        <p><strong>Being a student</strong> is the required part, introduced here by <strong>then</strong>. If you are enrolled in law school, being a student is certain. You cannot be enrolled without being a student. That is why <strong>being a student is necessary for law-school enrollment</strong>.</p>\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation\\" aria-label=\\"Why a requirement is not necessarily enough\\">\\r\\n          <h3>Required doesn\'t mean enough</h3>\\r\\n          <p>Knowing that a condition is required doesn\'t, by itself, tell you whether it\'s enough.</p>\\r\\n          <p>Someone could be a history student without being enrolled in law school. So being a student is required for law-school enrollment without guaranteeing it.</p>\\r\\n          <p>The two conditions have different jobs: <strong>enrollment guarantees being a student; being a student is what enrollment requires.</strong> Knowing only that someone is a student doesn\'t tell you which subject they study.</p>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n        <NecessaryConditionExperience/>\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation sc-indicator-list\\" aria-label=\\"Group 2 indicator words\\">\\r\\n          <h3>Group 2: Necessary-condition indicators</h3>\\r\\n          <p>These words and phrases can help you recognize a requirement:</p>\\r\\n          <ul>{[\'only\', \'only if\', \'only when\', \'only where\', \'always\', \'requires\', \'must\'].map(word => <li key={word}>{word}</li>)}</ul>\\r\\n          <p>For example, “Law-school enrollment <strong>requires</strong> being a student.” Requires points to <strong>being a student</strong>, the condition that cannot be missing. Read the full relationship; a familiar word alone doesn\'t tell you what is required.</p>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n        <section className=\\"cr-sufficient-explanation nc-checks\\" aria-label=\\"Apply the necessary condition to a new rule\\">\\r\\n          <h3>Find the requirement in a new rule</h3>\\r\\n          <blockquote>An application is accepted for review only if it includes a current reference letter.</blockquote>\\r\\n          <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"What is required for an application to be accepted for review?\\" options={[\'Including a current reference letter.\', \'Including any reference letter, even an outdated one.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Only if identifies including a current reference letter as required. An application accepted for review cannot be missing that current letter.\', \'Current matters. An outdated letter does not meet the requirement in this rule. Keep that word when you identify what is required.\']}/>\\r\\n          <h3>Does meeting the requirement guarantee acceptance?</h3>\\r\\n          <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"Does including a current reference letter guarantee that the application is accepted for review?\\" options={[\'Yes. The letter is all the application needs.\', \'No. The letter is required, but the rule does not say it is enough.\']} correct={1} feedback={[\'You turned a requirement into a guarantee. The rule tells us that accepted applications include a current letter, not that every application with one is accepted.\', \'An application with a current letter could be accepted or not accepted. Both fit the rule. Having the required letter alone does not establish acceptance.\']}/>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n        <aside className=\\"cr-sufficient-bridge\\"><h3>Next: Put the relationship into a diagram</h3><p>We now have both roles: the condition that guarantees, and the condition it requires. Next, we\'ll use an arrow to record that connection.</p></aside>\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n      <p>Next, record the relationship with the must arrow.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={5} label=\\"The necessary family\\" title=\\"Listen for what the sentence needs\\">\\r\\n      <p>Return to our locker rule. Borrowing a locker requires a key. The next expressions all identify that requirement in their complete sentences.</p>\\r\\n      <WordFamily entries={necessaryFamily} role=\\"necessary\\"/>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-contrast\\"><div><h3>If</h3><p>“You borrow a locker <strong>if</strong> you have a key.”</p><p>Having a key is enough for borrowing under this sentence.</p></div><div><h3>Only if</h3><p>“You borrow a locker <strong>only if</strong> you have a key.”</p><p>Having a key is required. This sentence has not made it enough.</p></div></div>\\r\\n      <ReferenceQuestion prompt=\\"A guest enters the lounge only if the guest has a current pass. What does entering establish?\\" options={[\'The guest has a current pass.\', \'Every guest with a pass enters.\']} correct={0} feedback={[\'Only if identifies the current pass as required for entry. An entering guest therefore meets that requirement.\', \'You made the requirement enough. A guest could have a current pass and remain outside without violating the sentence.\']}/>\\r\\n      <details className=\\"cr-extension\\"><summary>More Group 2 forms, with the sentence intact</summary><p><strong>Only when</strong> and <strong>only where</strong> can restrict the occasions or places of an outcome: “The fountain runs only when its pump is on.” A running fountain requires an operating pump. The pump being on does not establish that the fountain runs.</p><p><strong>Requires</strong> directly names a requirement: “Entry requires a current pass.” Preserve current. <strong>Always</strong> needs the sentence\'s subject and scope: “The upstairs room is always quiet when the door is shut.” Here a shut door establishes quiet, not the other way around.</p><p><strong>Only</strong> and <strong>the only</strong> are different complete expressions. “Only people who get a bicycle win this raffle” and “The only people who win this raffle are people who get a bicycle” both preserve the rule: winning establishes getting a bicycle.</p></details>\\r\\n      <p>We can now recognize both roles before writing any symbols. Next, let\'s see what happens when a requirement is missing.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={6} label=\\"A missing requirement\\" title=\\"What if the requirement is missing?\\" dark>\\r\\n      <ContrapositiveDiscovery/>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n\\r\\n    <ReferenceStep number={7} label=\\"The four situations\\" title=\\"Keep what follows separate from what stays open\\">\\r\\n      <p>Change the fact about your outcome while keeping the rule. Treat each fact as settled, not as a report that you are still waiting. Decide what it establishes before comparing the explanation.</p>\\r\\n      <FourCaseExplorer/>\\r\\n      <h3>Apply another rule</h3>\\r\\n      <p>The next two clips use a different statement: if you\'re enrolled in law school, you\'re a student. Now we add information about Maya and examine what that statement lets us conclude.</p>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"cr-sufficiency\\"><SufficientConditionFilm/></div>\\r\\n      <p>Starting from getting a bicycle and concluding that you win is the tempting <strong>reverse</strong>. Starting from not winning and concluding that you do not get a bicycle is the tempting <strong>inverse</strong>. Neither follows from this rule alone.</p>\\r\\n      <p>The contrapositive starts from <strong>not getting a bicycle</strong> and establishes <strong>not winning the raffle</strong>. Its reasoning depends on the missing required result, not on reversing whichever words happen to appear.</p>\\r\\n      <p>We now understand the relationship in words. Symbols will let us record that understanding without carrying every word of the sentence.</p>\\r\\n    </ReferenceStep>\\r\\n"}'}]

../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-carry-into-the-conditional-bootcamp)\\r\\n- [Accuracy and method boundaries](#accuracy-and-method-boundaries)\\r\\n- [Calibration examples and review](#calibration-examples-for-different-lsat-surfaces)\\r\\n- [A reusable writing brief](#a-reusable-writing-brief)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The evidence and coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. Every supplied section, FAQ entry, table, worked diagram, and drill item was read. HTML line references below refer to that unchanged archived file.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe document has six main sections and six subordinate headings. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML says its formatting was adjusted. We can examine what a pause, table, side note, or separated example does for learning. We cannot claim its font, spacing, color, or CSS exactly reproduces the original publication. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator\'s teaching voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The voice in one original passage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.\\r\\n\\r\\n> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn\'t been inspected would break the rule.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can\'t see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> We don\'t know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn\'t tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> That\'s the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the opening definition before the example, the small prediction, the explanation connecting the rule to the case, and the change in what the reader is given. The last sentence names a distinction the reader has just seen. The passage has a conversational rhythm because it follows a person\'s thoughts. Casual vocabulary alone would not accomplish that.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn an interactive lesson, pause after the first question and again after the inspection-stamp question. Reveal each explanation after the learner has had an opportunity to answer. The printed passage shows the whole progression for calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the teacher relates to the reader\\r\\n\\r\\n### The reader is beside the teacher\\r\\n\\r\\nThe narrator talks directly to someone doing the work. “Let\'s,” “you,” and “we” keep the reader involved. The writing seldom steps back to describe what students generally do. It asks this reader to notice, try, decide, or check something now.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe reader is treated as capable of understanding ordinary meaning. The teacher supplies support when a technical step is new. In the opening, the source first gives the literal relationship, then asks the reader to take its certainty seriously. Later, it encourages a guess before revealing the contrapositive. These are expectations of participation, not claims that everything should feel easy.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** speak to the reader without making their intelligence the subject. Do not copy a line such as “Because you\'re a thinking human.” Give them a manageable question and the information to answer it. If they miss it, explain the precise move that went wrong.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Confidence lives in the reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is decisive about a valid consequence. It also says “We don\'t know” when the information cannot settle a question. These two kinds of confidence belong together. Clear teaching can be certain about the limits of what is known.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe teacher does not apologize for asking the reader to think. The explanation makes the request reasonable. In the FAQ, the narrator acknowledges early mistakes and explains the value of practicing anyway. The tone combines patience with a specific demand: attempt the question, write the diagram, inspect the error.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c69db1","wall_time_seconds":0.6161339,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1200,"output":"import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen, within } from \'@testing-library/react\';\\r\\nimport { afterEach, expect, it } from \'vitest\';\\r\\nimport { OpeningCourseContent } from \'./OpeningCourseContent\';\\r\\nimport { MixedFamilyPractice, mixedFamilyItems } from \'./MixedFamilyPractice\';\\r\\n\\r\\nafterEach(cleanup);\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'teaches necessity before the visual and distinguishes a requirement from a guarantee in fresh practice\', () => {\\r\\n  const { container } = render(<OpeningCourseContent id=\\"necessary\\"/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.queryByText(/raffle|bicycle|breathing/i)).toBeNull();\\r\\n  const explanation = container.querySelector(\'.nc-lesson .cr-sufficiency-lead\')!;\\r\\n  expect(explanation).toHaveTextContent(\'Whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too.\');\\r\\n  const firstControl = container.querySelector(\'.nc-controls button\')!;\\r\\n  expect(explanation.compareDocumentPosition(firstControl) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\\r\\n  const questions = [...container.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\'.nc-checks .cr-question\')];\\r\\n  const correctAnswers = [0, 1];\\r\\n  questions.forEach((question, index) => {\\r\\n    const area = within(question);\\r\\n    const answers = area.getAllByRole(\'button\');\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(answers[1 - correctAnswers[index]]);\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\')).toHaveTextContent(\'Not quite.\');\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(answers[correctAnswers[index]]);\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\')).toHaveTextContent(\'Correct.\');\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(area.getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Try again\' }));\\r\\n    expect(answers[0]).toHaveFocus();\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\')).toBeEmptyDOMElement();\\r\\n  });\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'retains the full sufficient explanation and necessity bridge on the dedicated course page\', () => {\\r\\n  render(<OpeningCourseContent id=\\"sufficient\\"/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'Next: The Necessary Condition\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'Use an indicator to find the guarantee\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelectorAll(\'.cr-sufficient-checks [role=\\"status\\"]\')).toHaveLength(2);\\r\\n  expect(screen.queryByText(/raffle|bicycle|100%|breathing/i)).toBeNull();\\r\\n  expect(screen.queryByText(/Maya|The fact|Enough for what\\\\?/i)).toBeNull();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'video\')).toBeNull();\\r\\n  const guaranteeExplanation = screen.getByText(/Enrollment already guarantees it/);\\r\\n  const firstControl = document.querySelector(\'.sc-sentence-map button\')!;\\r\\n  expect(guaranteeExplanation.compareDocumentPosition(firstControl) & Node.DOCUMENT_POSITION_FOLLOWING).toBeTruthy();\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'explains the sufficiency errors and retains keyboard focus when a learner retries\', () => {\\r\\n  const { container } = render(<OpeningCourseContent id=\\"sufficient\\"/>);\\r\\n  const questions = [...container.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\'.cr-question\')];\\r\\n  const correctAnswers = [0, 1];\\r\\n  questions.forEach((question, index) => {\\r\\n    const area = within(question);\\r\\n    const answers = area.getAllByRole(\'button\');\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(answers[1 - correctAnswers[index]]);\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\').textContent).toMatch(/Not quite/);\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(answers[correctAnswers[index]]);\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\').textContent).toMatch(/Correct/);\\r\\n    const retry = area.getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Try again\' });\\r\\n    retry.focus();\\r\\n    fireEvent.click(retry);\\r\\n    expect(answers[0]).toHaveFocus();\\r\\n    expect(area.getByRole(\'status\').textContent).toBe(\'\');\\r\\n  });\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'places narrated case applications after condition roles have been taught\', () => {\\r\\n  render(<OpeningCourseContent id=\\"missing-requirement\\"/>);\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { name: \'Apply another rule\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n  expect(document.querySelector(\'video\')).toHaveAttribute(\'controls\');\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'Enough to know\' })).toBeVisible();\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nconst section = (name: string) => screen.getByRole(\'heading\', { level: 2, name }).closest(\'section\') as HTMLElement;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction attemptTranslation(item: HTMLElement, number: number) {\\r\\n  fireEvent.change(within(item).getByLabelText(`Your diagram for sentence ${number}`), { target: { value: \'My complete conditions and arrow\' } });\\r\\n  fireEvent.change(within(item).getByLabelText(`Your English readback for sentence ${number}`), { target: { value: \'If my left condition holds, my right condition holds.\' } });\\r\\n  fireEvent.click(within(item).getByRole(\'button\', { name: `Compare my translation for sentence ${number}` }));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'alternates sufficient and necessary sentences in the mixed set\', () => {\\r\\n  expect(mixedFamilyItems.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(6);\\r\\n  mixedFamilyItems.forEach((item, index) => expect(item.family).toBe(index % 2 === 0 ? \'sufficient\' : \'necessary\'));\\r\\n"}'}]
